). The remaining required variations are given below:

\displaystyle\delta_{-4}\boxast_{9}\!\big({\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}+\tfrac{1}{d-5}\bar{\jmath}_{d}\big)

\displaystyle=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(d-9)\big(\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}\big({\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}+\tfrac{1}{d-5}\bar{\jmath}_{d}\big)-\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\Upsilon}^{a}\bar{\nabla}_{d}^{\phantom{(1)}\!\!\!\!}\big({\sf E}^{(2)}_{a}+\tfrac{1}{d-5}\bar{\jmath}_{a}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\Upsilon}^{a}\bar{\nabla}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{a}+(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\nabla}^{b}\bar{\Upsilon}_{b})\bar{F}_{ad}-2(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\Upsilon}^{b})\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{db}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\tfrac{(d-5)}{2}\bar{P}_{d}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{\bar{\Upsilon}a}+2(d-4)\bar{P}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{ad}+2\bar{W}_{da\bar{\Upsilon}b}\bar{F}^{ab}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{\Upsilon}_{d})\bar{\jmath}^{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+2[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{F}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}{}^{a}]\,,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

\delta_{-4}\big(\bar{P}^{ab}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{bd}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{C}_{abd}\bar{F}^{ab}\big)=\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\Upsilon}^{b})\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{db}-3\bar{P}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}{}^{b}\bar{F}_{bd}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\bar{P}_{d}{}^{b}\bar{F}_{\bar{\Upsilon}b}-\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\nabla}^{b}\bar{\Upsilon}_{b})\bar{F}_{ad}-\bar{W}_{da\bar{\Upsilon}b}\bar{F}^{ab}\ \color[rgb]{0,0,0}\,.

It is not difficult to check that the coefficients are exactly those required for cancellation.
∎

Remark 7.10
.

When
d\neq 4,5,7,9
, we may define

{\sf E}^{(4)}:=\tfrac{1}{d-7}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\check{\sf E}^{(4)}\,.

Also, based on invariance alone, it is possible to add the tensor

\bar{W}_{dabc}\bar{\nabla}^{c}\bar{F}^{ab}+\frac{3}{2}\bar{C}_{abd}\bar{F}^{ab}

to
\check{\sf E}^{(4)}
since the above defines a section of

T^{*}\Sigma\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma[-4])
. In particular, when
d=7
, this tensor equals the manifestly conformally invariant divergence
\bar{\nabla}^{c}(\bar{W}_{dabc}\bar{F}^{ab})
. In Lemma
7.14
we establish that the coefficient of the above-displayed tensor must vanish upon requiring that
\check{\sf E}^{(4)}=0
when evaluated on conformally compact Yang–Mills connections.

\blacklozenge

Viewing
d
as a formal variable, Equation (
7.17
) has poles at
d=4,5,9
with respective residues

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{24}\operatorname{res}_{d=4}\check{\sf E}^{(4)}_{d}

\displaystyle=\big[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d},\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}\big]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta_{-4}}}{{\mapsto}}(d-4)\big[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d},{\sf E}^{(1)}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}\big]\,,

(7.19)

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{6}\operatorname{res}_{d=5}\check{\sf E}^{(4)}_{d}

\displaystyle=\>\>\>\>[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{\jmath}^{a}]\>\>\>\>\>\>\>{\hskip 0.85358pt}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta_{-4}}}{{\mapsto}}(d-5)[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{F}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}{}^{a}]\,,\

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{12}\operatorname{res}_{d=9}\check{\sf E}^{(4)}_{d}

\displaystyle=\>\>\>\>\>\>\boxast_{9}{\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}\>\>\>\>\>\>\,{\hskip 0.85358pt}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta_{-4}}}{{\mapsto}}(d-9)\big(\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}{\sf E}^{(2)}_{c}-\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\Upsilon}^{a}\bar{\nabla}_{c}{\sf E}^{(2)}_{a}\big)\,.

In the above we have also listed the (dimension
d
) linearized conformal variations of the corresponding tensor structures. These vanish in dimensions
4,5,9
respectively, and therefore these three tensors define conformal invariants in those dimensions.
In turn, this suggests we define the following three boundary operators by subtracting the relevant pole in
\check{\sf E}^{(4)}
and then setting
d
to the corresponding value.

\displaystyle{\hskip 0.85358pt}{\sf E}^{(4_{4})}_{d}(g,

\displaystyle A):=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}+9H\big({\sf E}^{(3)}_{d}-\boxast_{7}{\sf E}_{d}^{(1)}\big)-(7P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{45}{2}H^{2})\big({\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}-\bar{\jmath}_{d}\big)

\displaystyle+(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big(3\bar{\nabla}_{d}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}-4\bar{\nabla}_{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}\big)+2\big(9H^{3}\!+\!10P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}H-(\bar{\Delta}H)-H\bar{J}-\nabla_{\hat{n}}J\big){\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}

\displaystyle+\big((\bar{\nabla}^{a}\!P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})\!+\!9H{\hskip 0.85358pt}\!(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\!H)\big)\bar{F}_{ad}\!+\!\tfrac{6}{5}\Big(\!\!\boxast_{9}\!\!\big({\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}\!\!-\!\bar{\jmath}_{d}\big)\!\!+\!\!\bar{P}^{ac}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{cd}\!+\!\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\!\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}\!+\!\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{C}_{acd}\bar{F}^{ac}\!\Big)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{4}{5}\hat{k}_{b}+\tfrac{12}{5}[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{\jmath}^{a}]\,,

\displaystyle{\sf E}^{(4_{5})}_{d}(g,

\displaystyle A):=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}+9H\big({\sf E}^{(3)}_{d}-\tfrac{3}{2}\boxast_{7}{\sf E}_{d}^{(1)}\big)-(7P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{45}{2}H^{2}){\sf E}^{(2_{5})}_{d}

\displaystyle+(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big(3\bar{\nabla}_{d}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}-4\bar{\nabla}_{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}\big)+\big(19H^{3}+22P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}H-2(\bar{\Delta}H)-2H\bar{J}-2\nabla_{\hat{n}}J\big){\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}

\displaystyle+\big((\bar{\nabla}^{a}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})+9H(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big)\bar{F}_{ad}+8\big[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d},\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}\big]

\displaystyle+\tfrac{3}{2}\Big(\boxast_{9}\!{\sf E}^{(2_{5})}_{d}+\bar{P}^{ac}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{cd}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{C}_{acd}\bar{F}^{ac}\Big)+\tfrac{3}{2}\hat{k}_{d}\,,

and

\displaystyle{\sf E}^{(4_{9})}_{d}(g,

\displaystyle A):=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}+9H\big({\sf E}^{(3)}_{d}+\tfrac{3}{2}\boxast_{7}{\sf E}_{d}^{(1)}\big)-\tfrac{1}{4}(7P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{45}{2}H^{2})\bar{\jmath}_{d}

\displaystyle+(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big(3\bar{\nabla}_{d}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}-4\bar{\nabla}_{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}\big)+\big(23H^{3}+30P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}H-2(\bar{\Delta}H)-2H\bar{J}-2\nabla_{\hat{n}}J\big){\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}

\displaystyle+\big((\bar{\nabla}^{a}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})+9H(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big)\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{8}{5}\big[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d},\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}\big]

\displaystyle-\tfrac{3}{4}\big(\bar{P}_{da}\bar{\jmath}^{a}+2\bar{P}^{ab}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{bd}+(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}+\bar{C}_{abd}\bar{F}^{ab}\big)-\tfrac{3}{2}\hat{k}_{d}\,.

In these terms we have the following result.

Proposition 7.11
.

Let
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
be a dimension
d
Poincaré–Einstein structure
equipped with a smooth connection
\nabla^{A}
on
{\mathcal{V}}M
.
When
\nabla^{A}
is respectively subject to

\displaystyle[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a},{\sf E}^{(1)}_{b}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle d=4\,,

\displaystyle[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{F}^{ac}]\,

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle d=5\,,

\displaystyle{\sf E}^{(2)}_{a}\>\>\>\>

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle d=9\,,

then

{\sf E}^{(4_{j})}_{\,b}
defines a section of
T^{*}\Sigma\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma[-4]
when
d=j=4,5,9
.

Proof.

As explained above,
the formulæ for
{\sf E}^{(4_{4})}
,
{\sf E}^{(4_{5})}
,
{\sf E}^{(4_{9})}
in Equations (
), (
) and (
)
are obtained by removing, respectively, the poles

-\tfrac{8(d-7)}{24}\big[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}
,
\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}\big]
,

\frac{12(d-7)}{(d-5)(d-9)}[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{\jmath}^{a}]
,
and

-\frac{6(d-7)}{d-9}\boxast_{9}{\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}

from the expression for
\check{\sf E}^{(4)}
. It can be checked that the resulting expressions are not singular at
d=4,5,9
, respectively.

The proof now follows that for Proposition
7.9

quite closely. By construction, any poles in the formula for the variation of the leading term
\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}
are removable. We now examine the variations listed in Equation (
7.19
) of the residues of the poles that were subtracted to construct
{\sf E}^{(4_{4})}
,
{\sf E}^{(4_{5})}
and
{\sf E}^{(4_{9})}
. Then we
observe that the respective conditions placed upon
\nabla^{A}
in the proposition statement, imply vanishing of those variations. Cancellation of all remaining variations proceeds exactly along the lines presented in the proof of Proposition
7.9
.

∎

The case of
d=7
is of particular interest for
\check{\sf E}^{(4)}
. In particular note from Equation
7.14
that the
d=7
pole in the pair of terms

\big({\sf E}^{(3)}_{d}+\tfrac{3}{d-7}\boxast_{7}{\sf E}_{d}^{(1)}\big)
appearing in the first line of Equation (
7.17
) is removable. Hence, in
d=7
dimensions, Equation (
7.17
) determines an invariant expression for
\check{\sf E}^{(4)}

where it returns the
tensor
-6\hat{k}
. In dimension
d
,
the linearized conformal variation of
\hat{k}
is given by

(7.23)

\delta_{-4}\hat{k}_{b}=\tfrac{d-7}{2}\Big(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\bar{\nabla}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}\bar{\jmath}_{b}-\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{\Upsilon}^{a}\bar{\nabla}_{b}\bar{\jmath}_{a}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\nabla}^{c}\bar{\Upsilon}_{c})\bar{F}_{ab}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\Upsilon}_{b})\bar{\jmath}_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\Upsilon}^{c})\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{bc}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\\
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+\bar{P}_{b}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{\bar{\Upsilon}a}+(d-2)\bar{P}_{\bar{\Upsilon}}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{ab}+\bar{W}_{ba\bar{\Upsilon}c}\bar{F}^{ac}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Big)\,.

This establishes that
\hat{k}
is invariant in
d=7
dimensions; Theorem
1.11
shows that it equals the
d=7
obstruction current
\bar{k}
.
Just as in the case of
\check{\sf E}^{(2)}
when
d=5
, we consider the conformally compact Yang–Mills system with a flat boundary connection, in which case both
\hat{k}
and its conformal variation vanish. We may then construct an operator that extracts the transverse order four Neumann data. This operator is given below, and discussed further in Section
7.3
.

Proposition 7.12
.

Let
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
be a dimension
d=7
Poincaré–Einstein structure
equipped with a smooth connection
\nabla^{A}
on
{\mathcal{V}}M
.
When the boundary curvature induced by
\nabla^{A}
is subject to

\bar{F}_{ab}=0\,,

then

\displaystyle{\sf E}^{(4_{7})}_{d}(g,A)

\displaystyle:=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}+9H{\sf E}^{(3_{7})}_{d}-(7P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{45}{2}H^{2}){\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}

\displaystyle+(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big(3\bar{\nabla}_{d}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}-4\bar{\nabla}_{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}\big)+\tfrac{8}{3}\big[{\sf E}^{(1)}_{d},\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}\big]

(7.24)

\displaystyle+\big(21H^{3}+26P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}H-2\bar{\Delta}H-2H\bar{J}-2\nabla_{\hat{n}}J\big){\sf E}^{(1)}_{d}+3\boxast_{9}\!{\sf E}^{(2)}_{d}\,,

defines a section of
T^{*}\Sigma\otimes{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma[-4]
.

Proof.

The proof follows that of Proposition
7.11

mutatis mutandis
.
∎

As we shall see in Section
7.3
, for the boundary operator
{\sf E}^{(4_{7})}
to correspond to Neumann data, it must satisfy a divergence-free property.

Lemma 7.13
.

Let
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
be a dimension
d=7
Poincaré–Einstein structure.
Then if
\nabla^{A}
is a conformally compact Yang–Mills connection on
{\mathcal{V}}M
for which
\bar{F}_{ab}=F_{ab}|_{\Sigma}=0
,
then

\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\sf E}^{(4_{7})}_{a}=0\,.

Proof.

Using necessarily
(see Lemmas
7.3
and
7.8
)
that

(7.25)

0={\sf E}^{(3_{7})}={\sf E}^{(2)}={\sf E}^{(1)}=\bar{F}\,,

we have

\displaystyle{\sf E}^{(4_{7})}

\displaystyle=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}\,.

Returning to Equation (
7.16
), we obtain

0=n^{b}n^{c}\nabla_{n}\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c}\nabla^{d}F_{nd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla_{d}^{\top}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c}F_{n}{}^{d}\\
+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\hat{n}^{d}[\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c},n^{e}]F_{ed}+n^{a}n^{b}n^{c}[\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c},\nabla^{d}]F_{nd}\,.

Thus, combining the above two displays gives

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}^{d}{\sf E}^{(4_{7})}_{d}-[\nabla_{d}^{\top},n^{a}n^{b}n^{c}]\nabla_{a}\nabla_{c}F_{n}{}^{d}+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\hat{n}^{d}\nabla_{\hat{n}}[\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c},n^{e}]F_{ed}

\displaystyle+n^{b}n^{c}R_{n}{}^{d}{}{}^{\sharp}\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c}F_{nd}+n^{b}n^{c}\nabla_{n}[\nabla_{b}\nabla_{c},\nabla^{d}]F_{nd}\,.

We must now show that the last four terms on the right hand side
above vanish by virtue of Equation (
7.25
).
First observe that this equation implies that

0=(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}F_{ab}|_{\Sigma}=\hat{n}^{a}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}F_{ab}|_{\Sigma}=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}\nabla_{a}F_{bc}|_{\Sigma}\\
=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}\nabla_{a}F_{bc}|_{\Sigma}=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd}|_{\Sigma}\,,

for any positive integer
\ell
, and in turn

0=(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}F_{ab}|_{\Sigma}=(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}F_{nb}|_{\Sigma}=\hat{n}^{a}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}\nabla_{a}F_{nc}|_{\Sigma}\\
=\hat{n}^{a}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}\nabla_{a}F_{nc}|_{\Sigma}=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla^{\top})^{\ell}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{nd}|_{\Sigma}\,.

After developing commutators, we see that none of these four above-mentioned terms involves more than three derivatives of the curvature
F
, so it remains to establish that they can be expressed in terms of the above vanishing set.
This is easily achieved, once one realizes that the Bianchi identity can be used to write

n^{c}\nabla_{c}F_{ab}=-2\nabla_{[a}F_{b]n}+(\nabla_{[a}n^{c})F_{b]c}\,.

The last term above is lower order in derivatives of
F
while two or fewer derivatives of the first summand
can readily be expressed in terms of the vanishing set.
∎

It remains to establish the vanishing property of
{\sf E}^{(4)}
on solutions.

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
be a dimension
d\geq 8
Poincaré–Einstein structure.
If
\nabla^{A}
is a conformally compact Yang–Mills connection on
{\mathcal{V}}M
, then

\begin{array}[]{cl}{\sf E}^{(4)}_{a}=0\,,&d=8,10,11,12,\ldots\,,\\[5.69054pt]
{\sf E}^{(4_{9})}_{a}=0\,,&d=9\,.\end{array}

Proof.

We begin with the cases where
d\neq 9
. From Lemmas
7.3

and
7.8
we have

0={\sf E}^{(1)}={\sf E}^{(2)}={\sf E}^{(3)}\,,

so that

\displaystyle\check{\sf E}^{(4)}_{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(d-7)\Big[\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}-\tfrac{1}{d-5}(7P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{45}{2}H^{2})\bar{\jmath}_{d}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\big((\bar{\nabla}^{a}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})+9H(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big)\bar{F}_{ad}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\tfrac{6}{d-9}\Big(\tfrac{1}{d-5}\boxast_{9}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\bar{P}^{ab}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{bd}+\tfrac{1}{2}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{1}{2}\bar{C}_{abd}\bar{F}^{ab}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{12}{(d-5)(d-9)}[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{\jmath}^{a}]\Big]+\tfrac{12}{d-9}\,\hat{k}_{d}\,.

Now we develop the first term of the above display.

\displaystyle\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle F_{cd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{nd}-\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},n^{c}]F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{d-4}\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}(\sigma\nabla^{c}F_{cd})-\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},n^{c}]F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{d-4}\big(\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}\nabla^{c}F_{cd}+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}(n_{a}\nabla_{b}\nabla^{c}F_{cd})+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}(n_{b}\nabla^{c}F_{cd})\big)

\displaystyle-\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},n^{c}]F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{d-4}\big(3\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}\nabla^{c}F_{cd}+H\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla^{c}F_{cd}+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a},n_{b}]\nabla^{c}F_{cd}\big)

\displaystyle-\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},n^{c}]F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{d-7}\big(3\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{c}^{\top}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F^{c}{}_{d}+3\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},\nabla^{c}]F_{cd}+H\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla^{c}F_{cd}

\displaystyle\qquad+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a},n_{b}]\nabla^{c}F_{cd}-(d-4)\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},n^{c}]F_{cd}\big)\,.

Working term by term, the first two tensor structures above are handled as follows (again we rely heavily on the identities developed in Section
5
). In the following a
\top
above an equals sign denotes projection in hypersurface cotangent bundle directions.

\displaystyle\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\!\!

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\nabla_{c}^{\top}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\!\!F^{c}{}_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}\nabla_{c}^{\top}(\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}F^{c}{}_{d})-H\hat{n}^{b}\nabla^{\top}_{c}\nabla_{b}F^{c}{}_{d}-H\bar{g}^{bc}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\nabla}^{c}\bar{\nabla}_{[c}\bar{\jmath}_{d]}-2\bar{\nabla}^{c}(\bar{P}_{[c}{}^{b}\bar{F}_{d]b})+\big(2(\bar{\nabla}^{c}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})+10H(\bar{\nabla}^{c}H)\big)\bar{F}_{cd}

\displaystyle+(2P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+5H^{2})\bar{\jmath}_{d}+(d-2)H\Big(P_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\bar{F}_{dc}-\tfrac{5}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}H\bar{\jmath}_{d}\Big)

\displaystyle-2H\nabla^{\top}_{c}\big(\tfrac{1}{d-5}(\hat{n}^{c}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\hat{n}_{d}\bar{\jmath}^{c})-2H\bar{F}^{c}{}_{d}\big)+H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-H\bar{g}^{bc}[\nabla_{n},\nabla_{b}]F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\nabla}^{c}\bar{\nabla}_{[c}\bar{\jmath}_{d]}-2\bar{\nabla}^{c}(\bar{P}_{[c}{}^{b}\bar{F}_{d]b})+\big(2(\bar{\nabla}^{c}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})-(d+12)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\big)\bar{F}_{cd}

\displaystyle+(2P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{4d-41}{d-5}H^{2})\bar{\jmath}_{d}-HR_{\hat{n}b}{}^{be}\bar{F}_{ed}-HR_{\hat{n}bd}{}^{e}\bar{F}^{b}{}_{e}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle\tfrac{2}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\nabla}^{c}\bar{\nabla}_{[c}\bar{\jmath}_{d]}-2\bar{\nabla}^{c}(\bar{P}_{[c}{}^{b}\bar{F}_{d]b})+\big(2(\bar{\nabla}^{c}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})-15HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\big)\bar{F}_{cd}

\displaystyle+\big(2P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{4d-41}{d-5}H^{2}\big)\bar{\jmath}_{d}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\![\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},\nabla^{c}]\!\!\!\!\!\!

\displaystyle F_{cd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}R_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}{}_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\nabla_{e}^{\top}\bar{F}_{cd}+R_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}{}_{c}{}^{e}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ed}+R_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}{}_{d}{}^{e}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ce}

\displaystyle+\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\big([F_{b}{}^{c},F_{cd}]+R_{b}{}^{c}{}_{c}{}^{e}F_{ed}+R_{b}{}^{c}{}_{d}{}^{e}F_{ce}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}

\displaystyle P^{ce}\nabla^{\top}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}-P_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla^{\top}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}+P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\nabla^{\top}_{e}\bar{F}^{e}{}_{d}

\displaystyle-2(d-3)P_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ed}-2J\hat{n}^{e}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ed}-2P^{c}{}_{d}\hat{n}^{e}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ce}

\displaystyle+\hat{n}^{b}[(\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{b}{}^{c}),\bar{F}_{cd}]-\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}Ric_{b}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{ed}+\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}R_{b}{}^{c}{}_{d}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{ce}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}

\displaystyle P^{ce}\bar{\nabla}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}-HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\bar{F}_{ed}+HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\bar{F}_{ed}+P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}

\displaystyle-2(d-3)\big(\tfrac{1}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-2HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\bar{F}_{ed}\big)-\tfrac{2}{d-5}J\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\tfrac{2}{d-5}P_{cd}^{\top}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\jmath}^{c}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{d-5}[\bar{\jmath}^{c},\bar{F}_{cd}]\!-\!(d-2)\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}P_{b}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{ed}+\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}W_{b}{}^{c}{}_{d}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{ce}\!-\!\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}P_{b}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{de}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bar{P}^{ce}\bar{\nabla}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}+\tfrac{d+1}{2(d-5)}H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\tfrac{d+1}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+4(d-3)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\bar{F}_{ed}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{2}{d-5}\bar{J}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\tfrac{2}{d-5}\bar{P}^{c}{}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{c}+\tfrac{1}{d-5}[\bar{\jmath}^{c},\bar{F}_{cd}]-(d-3)\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}P_{b}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{ed}

\displaystyle+\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}W_{b}{}^{c}{}_{d}{}^{e})\bar{F}_{ce}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\bar{P}^{ce}\bar{\nabla}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}+\tfrac{d+1}{2(d-5)}H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\tfrac{d+1}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+6(d-3)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\bar{F}_{ed}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{2}{d-5}\bar{J}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\tfrac{2}{d-5}\bar{P}^{c}{}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{c}+\tfrac{1}{d-5}[\bar{\jmath}^{c},\bar{F}_{cd}]-(d-3)C_{\hat{n}}{}^{b}{}_{\hat{n}}\bar{F}_{bd}

\displaystyle-(d-3)(\bar{\nabla}_{e}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})\bar{F}^{e}{}_{d}+\bar{C}_{d}{}^{ec}\bar{F}_{ce}\,.

The third tensor structure is precisely that appearing in Equation (
5.33
) multiplied by
H
.

H\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla^{c}F_{cd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}-(d-4)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\bar{F}_{cd}-\tfrac{2(d-4)}{d-5}\,H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}\,.

The remaining two structures are then computed as below.

\displaystyle\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a},n_{b}]\nabla^{c}F_{cd}\!\!

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!2H\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla^{c}F_{cd}-\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}(P_{ab}+g_{ab}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})\nabla^{c}F_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!-2(d-4)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\bar{F}_{cd}-\tfrac{4(d-4)}{d-5}\,H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\tfrac{2(d-4)}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\jmath}_{d}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}[\nabla_{n}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b},n^{c}]F_{cd}\!\!

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!H\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}F_{cd}+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\big((-\sigma P_{a}{}^{c}-\delta_{a}^{c}\rho)\nabla_{b}F_{cd}\big)

\displaystyle+\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\big((-\sigma P_{b}{}^{c}-\delta_{b}^{c}\rho)F_{cd}\big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!3H\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}F_{cd}-2P_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{cd}-3P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\hat{n}^{a}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ad}

\displaystyle-\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}P_{b}{}^{c})F_{cd}-\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\nabla_{\hat{n}}(n_{a}P_{b}{}^{c}F_{cd})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!3H\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{b}F_{cd}-3P_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{cd}-3P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\hat{n}^{a}\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ad}

\displaystyle-2\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}P_{b}{}^{c})\bar{F}_{cd}-HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}\bar{F}_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!3HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{da}-\tfrac{15}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\tfrac{6}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+5HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{e}\bar{F}_{ed}

\displaystyle-2C_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}{}_{\hat{n}}\bar{F}_{cd}-2\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{a}(\nabla^{c}P_{ba})\bar{F}_{cd}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma,\top}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\!\!6HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{ad}\!-\!\tfrac{15}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}\!-\!\tfrac{6}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}\!-\!2(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\!P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})\bar{F}_{ad}\!-\!2C_{\hat{n}}{}^{c}{}_{\hat{n}}\bar{F}_{cd}\,.

Hence

\displaystyle(d-7)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}\tfrac{6}{d-5}\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{\nabla}_{[a}\bar{\jmath}_{d]}-6\bar{\nabla}^{a}(\bar{P}_{[a}{}^{c}\bar{F}_{d]c})-(d-7)(\bar{\nabla}^{a}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})\bar{F}_{ad}

\displaystyle+9(d-7)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{7(d-7)}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\tfrac{45(d-7)}{2(d-5)}H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\tfrac{3}{d-5}\bar{P}^{ce}\bar{\nabla}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}\!-\!\tfrac{6}{d-5}\bar{J}\bar{\jmath}_{d}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{6}{d-5}\bar{P}^{c}{}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{c}+\tfrac{3}{d-5}[\bar{\jmath}^{c},\bar{F}_{cd}]-(d-1)C_{\hat{n}}{}^{b}{}_{\hat{n}}\bar{F}_{bd}+3\bar{C}_{d}{}^{ec}\bar{F}_{ce}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{12}{d-5}\hat{k}_{d}-\tfrac{6(d-9)}{d-5}\bar{\nabla}^{a}(\bar{P}_{[a}{}^{c}\bar{F}_{d]c})+\tfrac{6}{d-5}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}-(d-7)(\bar{\nabla}^{a}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})\bar{F}_{ad}

\displaystyle+9(d-7)HP_{\hat{n}}{}^{a}\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{7(d-7)}{d-5}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+\tfrac{45(d-7)}{2(d-5)}H^{2}\bar{\jmath}_{d}+3\bar{P}^{ce}\bar{\nabla}_{e}\bar{F}_{cd}

\displaystyle+\tfrac{6}{d-5}\bar{P}^{c}{}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{c}+3\bar{C}_{d}{}^{ec}\bar{F}_{ce}\,.

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\tfrac{12}{d-5}\hat{k}_{d}+(d-7)\Big[\tfrac{3}{d-5}(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}+\tfrac{6}{d-5}\bar{P}^{ab}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{bd}+\tfrac{3}{d-5}\bar{P}^{c}{}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{c}+\tfrac{3}{d-5}\bar{C}_{abd}\bar{F}^{ab}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\tfrac{1}{d-5}(7P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}+\tfrac{45}{2}H^{2})\bar{\jmath}_{d}-\big((\bar{\nabla}^{a}P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}})+9H(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\big)\bar{F}_{ad}\Big]\,.

The proof is completed by employing the identity

(7.26)

\boxast_{9}\bar{\jmath}_{d}=4\hat{k}_{d}-4\bar{P}^{ab}\bar{\nabla}_{a}\bar{F}_{bd}-2(\bar{\nabla}^{a}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ad}-2\bar{C}_{abd}\bar{F}^{ab}+\tfrac{d-9}{2}\bar{P}^{c}{}_{d}\bar{\jmath}_{c}+2[\bar{F}_{ad},\bar{\jmath}^{a}]\,,

and remembering that
{\sf E}^{(4)}=\frac{1}{d-7}\check{\sf E}^{(4)}
in dimensions
8,10,11,12,\ldots
.
The special
d

=

9
case follows the same arguments as above with minor adjustments to handle the removable
d=9
singularities.
∎

The vanishing properties of boundary operators
{\sf E}^{(\ell)}
on solutions can be employed to compute expansion coefficients for the formal solution in Equation (
7.4
). Suppose, that
d\geq 8
and
\nabla^{A}
solves the conformally compact Yang–Mills equation (a similar analysis to that below can be applied when
d=5,6,7
, but in these cases fewer expansion coefficients
A^{(\ell)}
can be fixed). Then from Lemmas
7.3
,
7.8
, and
7.14

we have

(7.27)

0={\sf E}^{(1)}={\sf E}^{(2)}={\sf E}^{(3)}={\sf E}^{(4)}\,.

(When
d=9
, we must replace
{\sf E}^{(4)}
by
{\sf E}^{(4_{9})}
in the above).
Then, using Equation (
7.1
), we have that

F_{nb}{\rm d}y^{b}=\big(A^{(1)}_{j}(x)+2rA^{(2)}_{j}(x)+3r^{2}A^{(3)}_{j}(x)+4r^{3}A^{(4)}_{j}(x)\big){\rm d}x^{j}+{\mathcal{O}}(r^{4})\,.

Now
{\sf E}^{(1)}_{b}=F_{nb}|_{\Sigma}
, which implies

A^{(1)}_{j}(x)=0\,.

Turning to Equation (
7.11
) we learn
\hat{n}^{a}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ab})|_{\Sigma}=\frac{1}{d-5}{\hskip 0.85358pt}{\bar{\jmath}}_{b}
. But using
{\sf E}^{(1)}=0
and
\hat{n}_{a}{\rm d}y^{a}={\rm d}r
, we have

{\rm d}y^{b}\hat{n}^{a}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{ab})|_{\Sigma}={\rm d}y^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}F_{nb})|_{\Sigma}=2A^{(2)}_{j}{\rm d}x^{j}
,
whence

(7.28)

A^{(2)}_{j}(x)=\tfrac{1}{2(d-5)}{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\nabla}^{i}\bar{F}_{ij}\,.

Observe that the residue of the
d=5
pole recovers the
d=5
obstruction current.

A similar line of argument, now based on Equation (
7.14
) and the fact that
H=0
for the bulk metric representative (
5.25
), gives

A^{(3)}_{j}(x)=0\,.

Note that the vanishing of
A^{(1)}
and
A^{(3)}
also follows from our earlier discussion of the evenness property of
A^{\rm ev}
. The above result for
A^{(2)}
can also be quickly obtained by a direct Fefferman–Graham type order by order computation.

The fourth order coefficient
A^{(4)}
is significantly more involved. For that we turn to
the fourth boundary operator of Equation (
7.17
).
First, we note that in the
canonical compactified
metric
g
, we have that
n={\rm d}r
obeys
\nabla_{n}n=0
and
n^{2}=1
. In turn Lemma
5.2

implies that
\rho=0
and thus
P_{\hat{n}\hat{n}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Sigma}}{{=}}0
.
Hence, turning to Equation (
7.17
) in dimensions
d\geq 10
, or Equation (
) in dimension
d=9
, and using Equation (
7.27
) as well as Lemma
7.14
, allows us to extract
A^{(4)}
. Upon applying the identity
7.26
, we find

A^{(4)}_{k}(x)=\tfrac{1}{2(d-5)(d-7)}\Big(\hat{k}_{k}+\tfrac{1}{4}(d-7)\big(\bar{P}_{ji}\bar{\jmath}^{{\hskip 0.85358pt}i}+2\bar{P}^{ij}\bar{\nabla}_{i}\bar{F}_{jk}+(\bar{\nabla}^{i}\bar{J})\bar{F}_{ik}+\bar{C}_{ijk}\bar{F}^{ij}\big)\Big)\,.

7.3.
The Dirichlet-to-Neumann Map

We now study solutions of the
conformally compact Yang–Mills equation for Poincaré–Einstein structures beyond their leading asymptotics.
Firstly in the case
d
is even, there is no obstruction and we can apply Theorem
1.5
to find a
higher order asymptotic solution.
To analyze the Neumann data in that case, specializing to Poincaré–Einstein backgrounds, we
consider adding an order
r^{d-3}
term to
A^{\rm ev}
. The Ampère law (
7.3
) and vanishing of the obstruction for this dimension parity gives

j_{i}\big[A^{\rm ev}(x,r)+r^{d-3}A^{(d-3)}(x)\big]={\mathcal{O}}(r^{d-3})\,,

for
any

A^{(d-3)}(x)
.
However, the Gauß law (
7.2
) at order
r^{d-3}
does give a condition on
A^{(d-3)}(x)
, namely

(7.29)

\bar{\nabla}^{i}A^{(d-3)}_{i}=0\,.

An evenness argument shows that
the Neumann data
A^{(d-3)}
defines a conformal invariant:
When the formal solution
A_{(d-3)}:=A^{\rm ev}(x,r)+r^{d-3}A^{(d-3)}(x)
is re-expressed in terms of a conformally related boundary metric
\bar{\Omega}^{2}\bar{h}
, we have

A_{(d-3)}=A^{\rm ev}\big(x,\tilde{r}/\Omega(x,r)\big)+\tilde{r}^{d-3}\Omega(x,r)^{3-d}A^{(d-3)}(x)+{\mathcal{O}}(r^{d-2})
. Because the first term in this expression involves only even powers of
r
and
\tilde{r}=\Omega(x,r){\hskip 0.85358pt}r

where
\Omega(x,r)
has a suitably even expansion in
r
, it follows that
the coefficient of the
(d-3)^{\rm rd}
power of the defining function determines a section of
T^{*}\Sigma[3-d]\otimes{\operatorname{End}\mathcal{V}}\Sigma
.
Notice also that the divergence in Equation (
7.29
) is an invariant operation
when acting on (endomorphism-valued) one-forms of precisely this weight.

Now let
{\mathscr{A}}
denote the subset of the space of conformally compact Yang–Mills connections that obey
j[A]={\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{d-3})
.
Then we get a canonical
Dirichlet-to-Neumann
operator

{\mathscr{E}}^{(d-3)}:{\mathscr{A}}\to T^{*}\Sigma\otimes\operatorname{End}{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma[3-d]\,,

whose output is the section
A^{(d-3)}
in the kernel of the conformally invariant coupled divergence operator (meaning
\bar{\nabla}^{a}{\mathscr{E}}_{a}^{(d-3)}=0
) constructed above.
Here
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
is Poincaré–Einstein, but a similar construction is available on general conformally compact manifolds.
In the situations where
A
is the unique global solution to
j[A]=0
, this operator gives a Dirichlet-to-Neumann map from the boundary Dirichlet data
A^{(0)}(x)
to the non-locally determined Neumann data.

The above coordinate-based construction, while canonical, is somewhat indirect. It would be propitious to construct a boundary operator that directly extracts Neumann data from solutions.
Constructing this operator was a main aim of Section
7.2
, where candidate operators where constructed for even dimensions
d=4,6
.

Proposition 7.15
.

Let
d=4
or
6
and
(M^{d},\boldsymbol{c},\sigma,A,\bar{A})
be a Poincaré–Einstein structure
equipped with a smooth connection
\nabla^{A}

with boundary condition
\bar{\nabla}^{\bar{A}}
such that

j[A]={\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{d-3})\,.

Then the Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator is given by

{\mathscr{E}}^{(d-3)}=\frac{1}{(d-3)!}{\sf E}^{(d-3)}\,.

Proof.

When
d=4
, we only need examine the boundary operator
{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}

and verify that it extracts the expansion coefficient
A^{(1)}(x)
. But
{\sf E}^{(1)}_{a}=\hat{n}^{b}F_{ba}
so the result follows directly from (
7.1
).

For the
d=6
case we need to examine
{\sf E}^{(3)}_{a}
as given by Equation (
7.14
). Because the connection
\nabla^{A}
is asymptotically Yang–Mills, we have that
{\sf E}^{(2)}=0={\sf E}^{(1)}
. Hence

{\sf E}_{a}^{(3)}=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}F_{bc})|_{\Sigma}+5H{\hskip 0.85358pt}\bar{\jmath}_{c}-(\bar{\nabla}^{a}H)\bar{F}_{ac}\,.

In the Fefferman–Graham expansion for a Poincaré–Einstein metric, the mean curvature as measured by the compactified metric vanishes, so only the first term above remains. It not difficult to check that this then equals

3!A^{(3)}(x)
.
∎

Theorem
1.8
established conformal invariance of the renormalized Yang–Mills action
S^{\rm ren}[A,\tau]
evaluated on a conformally compact Yang–Mills solution in even dimensions
d
. One can also consider variations along such solutions determined by a path of boundary connections. By construction, the result of this variation must be conformally invariant, divergence-free and probe asymptotics of the interior connection at order
d-3
. Indeed, the critical boundary operators
{\sf E}^{(d-3)}
could be defined this way.
Uniqueness of the expression found for the
d=4,6
cases
{\sf E}^{(1)}
and
{\sf E}^{(3)}
, respectively, establishes that these must be functional gradients of the renormalized Yang–Mills action.
We leave the problem of determining/characterizing such variations in higher dimensions to a comprehensive study of the renormalized Yang–Mills action functional.

As mentioned above, when given data
(M^{d},\boldsymbol{c},\sigma,\bar{A},{E})
for
d=4,6,8,\ldots
, and a conformally compact structure that is Poincaré–Einstein, then Theorem (
1.5
) ensures a canonical
{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{\infty})
solution to Problem
1.1
.

It now remains to consider
d
odd.
In that case the above evenness argument no longer applies and instead Proposition
4.5
implies that when
the obstruction current is non-vanishing,
a polyhomogeneous expansion (involving both powers of
r
and
\log r
) is required for the higher order solution asymptotics. However, once the obstruction current
\bar{k}
vanishes, one might hope for a result along the lines of Proposition
7.15
. Here there is an essential distinction to the case of
d
even. While vanishing of the obstruction current
\bar{k}
does guarantee the absence of
log terms in the expansion of
A_{i}(x)
, this
alone does not suffice to ensure that the coefficient
A_{i}^{(d-3)}(x)
capturing the Neumann data defines a conformal invariant. This is because a new choice of
\bar{g}\in\boldsymbol{c}_{\Sigma}
changes the higher even order coefficients in the connection expansion which now include
A^{(d-3)}_{i}(x)
.

Proposition
4.5
suggests, when
d
is odd and the obstruction current
k_{i}(x)\neq 0
, that

j_{i}\big[A^{\rm ev}(x,r)+r^{d-3}\big(\tfrac{1}{d-3}{\hskip 0.85358pt}k(x)\log r+A^{(d-3)}(x)\big)\big]={\mathcal{O}}(r^{d-3}\log r)+{\mathcal{O}}(r^{d-3})\,.

Indeed the above is easily verified. Also the Gauß law at order
r^{d-3}
still implies that
\bar{\nabla}^{i}A^{(d-3)}_{i}=0
(recall that the obstruction obeys
\bar{\nabla}^{i}k_{i}=0
).
Thus, given the data
of any solution
A
to the conformally compact Yang–Mills system to order
j[A]={\mathcal{O}}(r^{d-3})
(with
d
odd and
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
Poincaré–Einstein), given
\bar{g}\in\boldsymbol{c}_{\Sigma}
we get a canonical
Dirichlet-to-Neumann operator

(7.30)

{\mathscr{E}}^{(d-3)}_{\bar{g}}:\mathscr{A}\to T^{*}\Sigma\otimes\operatorname{End}{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma\,,

whose output, given the choice of boundary metric representative
\bar{g}
, is the section
A^{(d-3)}
(in the kernel of the coupled divergence operator) constructed above.
In the above
{\mathscr{A}}
again denotes the subset of the space of conformally compact Yang–Mills connections that obey
j[A]={\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{d-3})
.
The situation simplifies when the boundary connection
\bar{A}
is flat.

Proposition 7.16
.

Let
d\geq 5
be odd and
(M^{d},\boldsymbol{c},\sigma,A,\bar{A})
be a Poincaré–Einstein structure
equipped with a smooth connection
\nabla^{A}

and boundary condition
\bar{\nabla}^{\bar{A}}
such that

j[A]={\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{d-3})\,.

Then, when the curvature of the boundary connection obeys

\bar{F}=0\,,

it follows that
{\mathscr{E}}^{(d-3)}_{\bar{g}}
defines a section of
\operatorname{End}{\mathcal{V}}\otimes T^{*}\Sigma[3-d]
given for
d=5,7
by

{\mathscr{E}}^{(d-3)}=\tfrac{1}{(d-3)!}\,{\sf E}^{\left((d-3)_{d}\right)}\,.

Proof.

To establish that
Equation (
7.30
) defines a conformally invariant map we first note that by Equation (
7.28
) and flatness of the boundary connection, we have
A^{(2)}=0
. The recursion determined by Equation (
7.6
) then implies that
A^{(4)}=A^{(6)}=\cdots=A^{(d-5)}=0
.
Hence
A^{\rm ev}
is independent of
r
so
A^{(d-3)}
cannot depend on the choice of boundary metric representative
\bar{g}
.

It remains to study the special cases
d=5,7
.
When
d=5
, we must examine

{\sf E}^{(2_{5})}
as given in Equation (
7.13
). Since
{\sf E}^{(1)}=0
(because
\nabla^{A}
is asymptotically Yang–Mills) we obtain

{\sf E}^{(2_{5})}_{\,c}(g,A)=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}(\nabla_{a}F_{bc})|_{\Sigma}\,.

It is easy to check that the above equals
2A^{(2)}
when evaluated in the
canonical connection expansion.

For
d=7
, we consider Equation (
7.24
)
and use Equation (
7.25
), which gives

\displaystyle{\sf E}^{(4_{7})}

\displaystyle=\hat{n}^{a}\hat{n}^{b}\hat{n}^{c}(\nabla_{\hat{n}}\nabla_{a}\nabla_{b}F_{cd})|_{\Sigma}\,.

Again it is not difficult to check that the above equals
4!A^{(4)}
when evaluated in the canonical connection expansion.
∎

Once again, but now for
d
odd,
in situations where

A
is the unique global solution to
j[A]=0
, the operator of (
7.30
),
and its conformally invariant refinement for Yang–Mills flat boundaries of the above proposition, gives a Dirichlet-to-Neumann map from the boundary Dirichlet data
A^{(0)}(x)
to the non-locally determined Neumann data.

When the obstruction current
\bar{k}=0
, then
given data
(M^{d},\boldsymbol{c},\sigma,\bar{A},\bar{g},{E}_{\bar{g}})
for
d=5,6,9,\ldots
, where the conformally compact structure
(M,\boldsymbol{c},\sigma)
is Poincaré–Einstein,
\bar{\nabla}^{\bar{A}}
is a boundary connection,
\bar{g}\in\boldsymbol{c}_{\Sigma}
and
E_{\bar{g}}
is a divergence-free section of
\operatorname{End}{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma\otimes T^{*}\Sigma
,
Theorem
1.5
ensures a canonical
{\mathcal{O}}(\sigma^{\infty})
solution to Problem
1.1
.
In the special case where the curvature
\bar{F}
of

\bar{A}
is zero, we may refine the data

{E}_{\bar{g}}
to the conformal invariant
{\ E}\in\Gamma(\operatorname{End}{\mathcal{V}}\Sigma\otimes T^{*}\Sigma[3-d])
, now subject to a conformally invariant divergence condition
\bar{\nabla}^{a}{E}_{a}=0
.
This statement is easily established by observing that flatness of
\bar{\nabla}^{\bar{A}}
in fact implies
A^{\rm ev}(x,r)=0
. In that case one is studying the natural global analog of the formal electric problem of Section
4.3
.
Of course, vanishing of
\bar{F}
along
\Sigma
need not imply that
\bar{\nabla}^{\bar{A}}
is trivial.
